- **Cross-Compilation Dockerfile** — new `Dockerfile.cross` for building ocode binaries targeting multiple platforms from a single build context. (`Dockerfile.cross`)
- **Desktop App Icon** — new `appicon.png` source artwork, Windows `winres.json` with multi-size icon support (16px + 1024px), and pre-generated `rsrc_windows_{386,amd64}.syso` COFF objects. (`cmd/ocode-desktop/appicon.png`, `cmd/ocode-desktop/winres/`)
- **Debug Helper** — new `debug.ts` module for web UI debugging utilities. (`web/src/debug.ts`)
- **Design Specs** — added `2026-08-18-debug-helper-design.md` and `2026-08-19-desktop-sharing-design.md`. (`docs/superpowers/specs/`)

### Changed
- **Per-Project LSP Managers** — the single process-wide LSP manager (`sharedLSPManager`) is replaced by `lspManagerFor(root)` — one manager per project root, shared across tabs on the same repo. Sessions on different registered projects get language servers rooted at their own repo; the LSP status endpoint aggregates across all managers. (`internal/server/handler.go`)
- **Context-Aware Orphan Recovery** — `recoverOrphanedToolCalls` now receives a `stopCh` and passes `context.Background()` through `handleToolCallWithContext`, so `ContextualTool` implementations (bash, formatters) stop when recovery is cancelled or times out. An `orphanRecoveryWG` WaitGroup on `Agent` tracks abandoned recovery goroutines so `Shutdown` can wait before resetting shared state. (`internal/agent/agent.go`, `internal/agent/subagent.go`)
- **Platform-Specific Process Groups** — `SysProcAttr.Setpgid` calls are replaced by `setProcGroup(c)` in platform-specific files (`proc_unix.go` / `proc_windows.go`) across `internal/shell`, `internal/tool`, and `internal/server`, removing the `syscall` import from cross-platform code. (`internal/shell/shell.go`, `internal/shell/proc_unix.go`, `internal/tool/proc_unix.go`, `internal/server/proc_unix.go`)
- **Terminal Process Stats Emitter** — new `terminalProcsEmitter` streams per-terminal CPU/memory usage via SSE, reading from the `terminalRegistry`. (`internal/server/emitters.go`)

### Fixed
- **Muse Spark Tool Calls Routed to the Responses API** — `opencode-go/muse-spark-1.2` (and `-contributor`) previously went down the chat/completions path, where the zen proxy streams tool-call arguments that arrive as invalid JSON (`openai: invalid tool call arguments for glob`). A new `opencodeResponsesOnlyModel` predicate routes Responses-only zen models (GPT-5.6 family + `muse-spark*`) through `chatOpenAIResponses`; Muse Spark stays non-lite (no codex responses-lite header/developer-item tools, which remain GPT-5.6-only). The Responses SSE parser also now accumulates `response.function_call_arguments.delta` per item id as a fallback for providers whose `response.output_item.done` omits `arguments` — the done item's final arguments still win when present, and recovered arguments are written back into the stored item replayed as next-turn input. Verified live: muse-spark-1.2 completes a `glob` tool call end-to-end. (`internal/agent/client.go`, `internal/agent/codex_lite_test.go`, `internal/agent/agent_test.go`)
- **Advisor Config Empty vs Not-Provided** — `HandleSetAdvisor` now uses `*string` for the model field, so sending `{"model":""}` clears the advisor model while omitting `model` entirely leaves it unchanged. Previously the empty string was indistinguishable from "not provided". (`internal/server/handler_config.go`)
- **File Tree Hides Generated/Vendored Directories** — `buildFileTree` now skips `node_modules`, `vendor`, `dist`, `build`, `target`, `.next`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`, and other well-known large directories, preventing the tree from being dominated by non-navigable content. (`internal/server/handler_files.go`)

### Changed
- **Version Bump** — 0.8.54 → 0.8.55: `/btw` runs an independent agent loop with tool calls on its own client; its popup body is scrollable and wrapped. (`internal/version/version.go`)
- **`/btw` Runs an Independent Agent Loop with Tools** — `/btw <message>` no longer fires a one-shot completion on the shared main client. It now builds a dedicated child agent (`Agent.AskLoopAsync` in `internal/agent/ask.go`) with its OWN fresh client, its own message list, and a tool set minus interactive/dispatch tools (`question`, `task`/`task_status`/`agent_status`/`task_cancel`, `wait`, `todo_write`/`todo_update`, `plan_enter`/`plan_exit`, `discover_more`, `knowledge_lookup`, `advisor`) — the exclusion is enforced on the child's FINAL tool map because `NewAgent` registers the dispatch family unconditionally — so the side query can read/grep/search the workspace and run bash to answer the aside, and can never race the main turn's installed `OnDelta`/`OnUsage` callbacks on a shared client. The child shares the parent's snapshot store and changes registry (side-query edits and bash mutations land in the Changes tab and stay undoable), shares the PermissionManager but denies `PermissionAsk` non-blockingly (the popup can't show dialogs; auto-permission still runs first when enabled), inherits redaction (registry, enabled state, tier-1 net hook re-applied onto its own client, tier-2 scanner), runs with discovery skipped and an 8-step cap, and tracks its bash processes under the shared supervisor with a `btw-N-` ID prefix. The popup streams live tool activity (`→ read: …`) and streamed text while the loop runs, then the final answer; Enter/Esc (dismiss), `/new`, and a new `/btw` cancel the running loop and kill only the child's own processes (never the shared supervisor's). Stale-generation handling, non-mutation of `m.messages`/session history, and queued execution (runs after the current stream ends — the child's own client removes the old OnDelta-race reason, but the popup blocks input while open and a second concurrent loop would interleave with the main turn) are unchanged. (`internal/agent/ask.go`, `internal/tui/model.go`, `internal/tui/btw_test.go`, `internal/agent/ask_test.go`)
- **`/btw` Popup Is Scrollable With Wrapped Text** — the popup body (aside question, live tool activity, streamed/final answer) renders inside a vertically scrollable viewport (`btwViewport`): long lines wrap at the dialog content width (the bubbles viewport does not soft-wrap, so `wrapView` runs before `SetContent`), the body height is capped at ~40% of the terminal (`btwDialogMaxBodyLines`, with the same short-terminal safety net as the permission dialog so the frame can never push past the screen height), the scroll offset survives live-streaming updates (no yank to top), and Up/Down/j/k/PageUp/PageDown plus the mouse wheel (over the dialog only) scroll it. A scroll indicator (▼/▲▼/▲) appears on the last visible line when content overflows; the dialog participates in `inputAreaHeight` so mouse hit-testing and chrome budgeting agree with what is painted. (`internal/tui/model.go`, `internal/tui/btw_test.go`)

### Changed
- **Version Bump** — 0.8.53 → 0.8.54: bundled auto-permission prompt addendum with `/permissions auto prompt` management. (`internal/version/version.go`)

### Added
- **Bundled Auto-Permission Prompt Addendum** — a versioned, installable system-prompt addendum is now prepended to the LLM auto-permission gatekeeper prompt. The installed file (`auto-permission-prompt.md`, alongside `ocodeconfig.json`) ships a default body of known-safe git commands that should always be allowed without further reasoning (`git status`/`diff`/`log`/`show`/`blame`/`ls-files`/`branch` (listing only, `-a`/`-v`/`-r`)/`remote -v`/`stash list`/`rev-parse`/`describe`/`fetch`/`add`), and is managed through the new `/permissions auto prompt <status|install|upgrade> [force]` subcommand: `status` reports `missing`/`up-to-date`/`outdated`/`custom-modified` (a `.bundled-hash` sidecar records the last installed version), `install` writes the bundled body (upgrading an untouched outdated copy automatically), and a custom-modified file is never overwritten unless `force` is passed — an existing copy is backed up (`.bak.<ts>`) first and the write is atomic (temp file + rename). The addendum is deliberately separate from `permissions.auto.prompt` in `ocodeconfig.json`: that field is the user's own free-form override and must never be silently clobbered by a bundled update. (`internal/config/auto_permission_prompt.go`, `internal/agent/agent.go`, `internal/tui/commands.go`)

### Changed
- **Version Bump** — 0.8.52 → 0.8.53: advisor-review follow-ups — `/btw` no longer runs mid-stream, web queue survives the temp-tab → session rekey, advisor dialog preserves the Claude Code toggle, up-arrow restore is message-only again. (`internal/version/version.go`)
- **`/btw` Queues Behind an Active Turn** — `/btw`/`/by-the-way` is removed from the instant-command list. It starts a one-shot LLM completion on the shared client, and running it mid-stream would race the main turn's installed `OnDelta` callback on the `GenericClient`, leaking the side-query's tokens into the main transcript/UI. Queued, it runs after the stream ends — naturally serialized. (Instant list + rationale in `internal/tui/model.go` and `AGENTS.md`.)
- **Up-Arrow Queue Restore Is Message-Only Again** — both the TUI and the web/desktop input recall the most recently queued *input* item, skipping queued commands: a command is waiting to run, and recalling it into the input would silently remove it from execution. Web `popLastQueued` now walks backward past `command` items, mirroring the TUI's restored backward walk. Locked in by `TestUpArrowRestoreSkipsQueuedCommands` / `TestUpArrowRestoreWithOnlyQueuedCommandsRestoresNothing` and the new `tabQueue.test.ts`. (`internal/tui/model.go`, `internal/tui/model_test.go`, `web/src/lib/tabQueue.ts`, `web/src/lib/tabQueue.test.ts`)
- **Model Dialog Advisor Pick Preserves the Claude Code Toggle** — the advisor branch of `ModelDialog` now fetches the current advisor config on open and carries `claude_code` through `setAdvisorFull`, so the server's provider-change convention (`provider` set → `claude_code = (provider === "claude-code")`) can no longer silently flip a toggle the user set explicitly in `AdvisorForm`. (`web/src/components/Layout/ModelDialog.tsx`)

### Fixed
- **Web Queued Input Survives the Temp-Tab → Session Rename** — `ChatInput` keys its queue by the temporary `new-*` tab id, but only chat/project state was rekeyed when the session was created, so anything typed while the first turn streamed was orphaned under the old id and never drained. `rekeyQueue(oldId, newId)` in `tabQueue.ts` moves the queue during `handleSessionCreated` (alongside `REKEY_SESSION`/`UPDATE_TAB_ID`; idempotent, so the SSE-driven and callback-driven paths can both fire), and closing a tab now `clearQueue`s it. (`web/src/lib/tabQueue.ts`, `web/src/App.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/OpenSessionBar.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/SessionDialog.tsx`)

### Changed
- **Version Bump** — 0.8.51 → 0.8.52: `/btw` side-query popup, single-purpose model dialog + Settings conversions, web/desktop input queueing, local-model tier-2 redaction scanning, discovery corpus & skill-loader fixes. (`internal/version/version.go`)
- **Model Dialog Is Single-Purpose, Settings Fields Open It Directly** — `ModelDialog` no longer has tabs; it selects for exactly one `purpose` at a time (`main`/`small`/`advisor`/`recap`/`ocr`/`mask`/`commit`/`summary`) with a purpose-specific title. Advisor picks persist immediately through `api.setAdvisorFull` using the registry's `{model, provider}` pair (`advisorSelectionPayload`); form-owned purposes (recap/ocr/mask/commit/summary) hand the pick back via `onPick` so the owning form persists it with its own Save — the dialog never writes those itself (a direct write would bypass the form's other fields and desync it). `currentValues` highlights the active model for form-owned purposes. Every Settings model field (Advisor, Commit Message, Compact Summary, Model Defaults Recap, OCR, Mask) is now a read-only display + "Change…" button that opens the dialog with the right purpose. (`web/src/components/Layout/ModelDialog.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/modelSelection.ts`, `web/src/components/Layout/modelSelection.test.ts`, `web/src/App.tsx`, `web/src/components/Settings/AdvisorForm.tsx`, `web/src/components/Settings/CommitMsgForm.tsx`, `web/src/components/Settings/CompactForm.tsx`, `web/src/components/Settings/ModelDefaultsForm.tsx`, `web/src/components/Settings/OcrForm.tsx`, `web/src/components/Settings/SecurityForm.tsx`)
- **Discovery Embedding Backend Defaults to Local** — `defaultDiscoveryConfig` now sets `EmbeddingBackend: "local"` instead of `"http"`, so a fresh install spins up the bundled embedder rather than silently pointing at an implicit remote server. (`internal/config/ocodeconfig.go`, `internal/config/discovery_config_test.go`)
- **TUI Up-Arrow Restores the Last Queued Item** — with the queue now unified (inputs and commands share `m.queuedItems` in insertion order), the empty-input up-arrow restore takes the last queued item regardless of kind instead of walking backwards to skip commands — the last queued item is always the most recently typed thing. (`internal/tui/model.go`)

### Added
- **`/btw` Side-Query Popup** — `/btw <message>` now runs a one-shot LLM completion over the current conversation history plus the aside text (`Agent.AskAsync`, a background, state-free `client.Chat` that records side usage) and shows the answer in a bordered popup above the input. Neither the aside nor the answer enters `m.messages` or the persisted session history; a monotonic generation counter discards stale responses, and the popup blocks normal input while open (enter/esc dismisses). (`internal/agent/ask.go`, `internal/tui/model.go`)
- **Web/Desktop Input Queueing** — typing while a turn is busy (streaming or a `!shell` command in flight) queues instead of blocking, mirroring the TUI's `queuedItems`: `/`-commands, `!`-shell commands, and plain messages (with refs/context baked in at queue time) queue per tab in `web/src/lib/tabQueue.ts`, auto-drain FIFO once the turn frees up (stopping as soon as an item starts a new turn), and ↑ in an empty box recalls the most recently queued item for editing. A queued-count line under the input shows the backlog. (`web/src/lib/tabQueue.ts`, `web/src/components/Chat/ChatInput.tsx`)
- **Local-Model Tier-2 Redaction Scanning** — the tier-2 LLM scanner now works with `/localmodel`-managed models end to end. `defaultRedactionBaseURL` assigns a `local/*` model its deterministic chat port (`discovery.AssignChatPort`, 11458+, with the `/v1` suffix the scanner's `/chat/completions` path needs), and `setRedactionModel` refreshes an auto-assigned URL when switching models or when the registered-model set changed, while preserving explicitly custom URLs. `buildLLMScanner` rewrites the persisted `local/<id>` to the server's actual launch id (`ChatManifest.ExpectedServeID`, e.g. the HF repo id) because `mlx_lm.server` honors the request `model` field as a live model-switch; a `local/*` id with no chat manifest for this host fails closed (regex tier-1 still applies, tier-2 skipped with a debug-logged reason) instead of sending a `local/<id>` an MLX server would choke on. The redaction-model picker also gains an enabled-local-models section (disabled entries can't be started, so they're excluded). (`internal/tui/redaction.go`, `internal/tui/picker.go`, `internal/tui/redaction_picker_test.go`)

### Fixed
- **Symlinked Skill Directories Loaded** — `loadSkillsFromPaths` no longer trusts `entry.IsDir()`, which is false for a symlink whose target is a directory; it stats through symlink entries so skill dirs installed as symlinks (the common layout under `~/.config/opencode/skills`) are discovered instead of silently skipped. (`internal/skill/loader.go`)
- **Discovery Status Denominator Is the Admitted Corpus** — `DiscoveryStatus.SkillTotal` now equals the names-index's actual contents (`len(AllSkills)`): every non-Kaizen skill plus the Kaizen skills admitted for the active model. Previously it counted all root skills, inflating the denominator with per-model-tuned skills gated out for the active model and making `/discover status`'s attached/total misleading. `discoveryDocs` also builds from the same root-aware load (`LoadSkillsForRoot`) and splits Kaizen out before the model gate. (`internal/agent/discovery_glue.go`, `internal/agent/discovery_glue_test.go`)

### Changed
- **Version Bump** — 0.8.50 → 0.8.51: `.claude/skills` (plural) project-local skill directory fix. (`internal/version/version.go`)
- **`.claude/skills` (Plural) Loaded as Project-Local Skill Dir** — `ProjectLocalSkillDirs` now searches `.claude/skills` (Claude Code's actual convention) instead of `.claude/skill`, so skills installed by Claude Code in that directory are discovered; the "none found" message in the learn context and the slash-command registration comment were updated to match. (`internal/skill/loader.go`, `internal/skill/learn.go`, `internal/tui/commands.go`)
- **Version Bump** — 0.8.49 → 0.8.50: multiproject event architecture Parts 02–06 — unified tagged event bus + `/api/events`, async session bootstrap with observable stages and turn lifecycle events, persist-then-202 dispatch, reconcile + per-session status endpoints, single frontend event transport, RC bridge first-class registration. (`internal/version/version.go`)
- **RC Bridge Sessions Registered First-Class in the Session Manager** — `Server.RegisterExternalSession` now registers the TUI's bridged session with the `SessionManager` (owning project + eviction exemption), so every session-scoped handler and the event bus resolve it instead of treating the RC bridge as a global "forward everything to the TUI" switch. `HandleSendMessage` routes by the session's registry entry: a bridged TUI session forwards to the bridge, a server session runs locally — no more unconditional TUI forwarding when any bridge is attached. Bridge frames are stamped with the real session id at source (`RCBridge.Broadcast` tags untagged frames with the bridge's own id and re-broadcasts every frame on the unified bus), and an untagged frame at the mirror is dropped loudly as a producer bug instead of being forwarded with an ambiguous tag. (`internal/server/rc_bridge.go`, `internal/server/handler.go`, `internal/server/handler_sse.go`, `internal/server/session_manager.go`, `internal/server/send_message_routing_test.go`)
- **Headless Runs Drop the `question` Tool** — `runcli.Run` removes the `question` tool from the tool list in non-interactive (`run`/`serve`) mode and appends a suffix telling the model not to call it, since no UI exists to answer — previously a `question` call in a headless run stalled the run. (`internal/runcli/run.go`)

### Added
- **Session-Scoped Debug Logs** — `debuglog.Entry` now carries an optional `SessionID`; every agent-family `emitDebug` call (agent loop, LLM client, permission manager, advisor checkpoints, doc/memory maintenance, discovery) routes through a session-tagged variant — `Agent.SetSessionID` tags the agent, its `PermissionManager`, and its `*GenericClient` at construction, and each `emitDebug` falls back to the process-global sink when untagged. `GET /api/logs` and `DELETE /api/logs` accept `?session_id=` to fetch/clear only that session's entries plus untagged process-global ones, and `logBusForwardLoop` publishes each entry with its `session_id` so the web `LogPanel` (now a per-tab `sessionId` prop) scopes fetch, stream, and clear to the active session. New `debuglog.ClearSession` removes one session's entries without resetting sequence numbers, so existing cursors stay valid. (`internal/debuglog/debuglog.go`, `internal/agent/agent.go`, `internal/agent/client.go`, `internal/agent/permissions.go`, `internal/agent/advisor_checkpoint.go`, `internal/agent/permission_interpreter.go`, `internal/agent/discovery_glue.go`, `internal/agent/doc_maintenance.go`, `internal/agent/memory_maintenance.go`, `internal/agent/md_discovery.go`, `internal/agent/group_bus.go`, `internal/server/agent_session.go`, `internal/server/handler_logs.go`, `internal/server/handler.go`, `web/src/components/Logs/LogPanel.tsx`, `web/src/App.tsx`)
- **Interactive Terminal Starts in the Tab's Project Directory** — `HandleTerminalWS` accepts a `project_path` query parameter and rejects anything that isn't a registered project root (the server workdir or a saved project — the same trust boundary session resolution uses, now shared via `Handler.allowedProjectRoots`), so the endpoint can never spawn a shell in an arbitrary directory. `TerminalPanel` pins each terminal socket to its tab's project, and the `useTerminalConfig` project-scope gate (which hid the terminal when the selected project wasn't the server workdir) is gone — directory choice moved per-connection. (`internal/server/handler.go`, `internal/server/handler_terminal.go`, `internal/server/handler_terminal_test.go`, `web/src/components/Terminal/TerminalPanel.tsx`, `web/src/components/Terminal/TerminalTabs.tsx`, `web/src/hooks/useTerminalConfig.ts`)
- **Unified Tagged Event Bus + `/api/events`** — A single server-side `EventBus` (`internal/server/event_bus.go`) now carries every event type (chat mirror frames, turn lifecycle, status, logs, agent runs, git status, spending) in one envelope: `{event, project, session_id, seq, data}` with a per-process monotonic `seq` for gap detection. `GET /api/events` is the single unified SSE endpoint; subscribers declare the projects they view (`?projects=`) which drives subscriber-aware emitters. Server-push emitters (`internal/server/emitters.go`: runs, git watchers, spending, logs) run only while at least one client is connected and self-exit ~30s after the last subscriber leaves. Every `broadcastEvent` headless frame is also published on the bus, tagged with the session's owning project from the registry. (`internal/server/event_bus.go`, `internal/server/handler_events.go`, `internal/server/emitters.go`, `internal/server/handler.go`, `internal/server/handler_sse.go`, `internal/server/handler_events_test.go`, `internal/server/event_bus_test.go`, `internal/server/emitters_test.go`)
- **Async Session Bootstrap with Observable Stages + Turn Lifecycle Events** — `buildAgentSession` (replacing the old `buildAgentSession` path) emits observable `session_bootstrap` stage events as it constructs an agent — `model` → `tools` → `mcp` → `ready` — and the MCP wait is now bounded (`mcpCache.waitTimeout`, 30s default): stragglers are dropped with a `session_bootstrap` warning event instead of hanging the first turn. Running turns emit `turn_started` / `turn_heartbeat` (10s interval) / `turn_done` / `turn_error` on the bus; a bootstrap failure emits `turn_error` carrying the failing stage. `POST /api/chat` and `POST /api/sessions/{id}/message` with `"async": true` now persist the user message to the session transcript before replying `202`, and run the turn (including any needed bootstrap) on a per-session goroutine — a bootstrap failure after `202` never loses the message. Per-session turn locks (`turnMu`/`turnLocks`) keep turns on one session strictly ordered while different sessions run in parallel. (`internal/server/agent_session.go`, `internal/server/session_manager.go`, `internal/server/handler.go`, `internal/server/handler_sse.go`, `internal/server/mcp_cache.go`, `internal/server/agent_session_turn_test.go`, `internal/server/auto_compact_test.go`, `internal/server/session_manager_state_test.go`)
- **Session Reconcile + Per-Session Status Endpoints** — `GET /api/sessions/{id}/state` is the reconcile endpoint: authoritative turn state (`bootstrap_stage`, `turn_active`) plus the bus `last_seq` watermark so the frontend can detect missed events on reconnect — reconcile is state fetch + transcript refetch, never event replay. `GET /api/sessions/{id}/status` returns a per-session status snapshot: a superset of the process-global `/api/tui-status` (model, advisor, OCR, cwd, LSP servers) plus the context data previously only on `/api/sessions/{id}/context`, with `session_id` populated and `cwd` set to the session's owning project root so each tab renders its own status without a TUI bridge. (`internal/server/handler_session_state.go`, `internal/server/handler_session_state_test.go`, `internal/server/session_manager.go`, `internal/server/session_manager_http_test.go`)
- **Per-Project Session Storage (`SaveForDir`/`LoadForDir`)** — Sessions can be saved and loaded under an explicit project root's storage dir (`session.SaveForDir`/`LoadForDir`, sharing a `saveToDir` core with `Save`), so multi-project servers persist each session under its owning project instead of the process workdir. (`internal/session/session.go`, `internal/session/session_for_dir_test.go`)
- **Frontend Single Event Transport** — The web app now consumes the unified bus through one long-lived `EventSource` on `/api/events` (`web/src/lib/eventBus.ts`, a singleton with reconnect + exponential backoff, `seq` gap detection that logs and fires reconcile handlers, and a declared project list driving the server's subscriber-aware emitters) with pure envelope routing into the stores extracted as `web/src/lib/sessionEvents.ts` (unit-testable without React). `SessionTabSync` is now a thin adapter feeding live envelopes into the router and firing `reconcileOpenSessions` on reconnect. The legacy per-session SSE connectors (`connectSessionMirror`, `connectAgentRunsSSE`) were deleted from the API client; `useAgentRuns` seeds itself with one fetch and updates from bus `runs` events. (`web/src/lib/eventBus.ts`, `web/src/lib/sessionEvents.ts`, `web/src/components/Layout/SessionTabSync.tsx`, `web/src/api/client.ts`, `web/src/hooks/useAgentRuns.ts`, `web/src/lib/eventBus.test.ts`, `web/src/lib/sessionEvents.test.ts`)
- **Per-Session Status Activation + Turn Watchdog** — `useSessionStatus` activates a session's status snapshot when its tab becomes active, and `useTurnWatchdog` clears a stuck streaming spinner by reconciling against `GET /api/sessions/{id}/state` when a `turn_started` isn't followed by `turn_done`/`turn_error` in time. Streaming state in `chatStore` now derives from per-session turn state (`SET_STREAMING` optimistically on the 202, confirmed by `turn_started`, cleared by `turn_done`/`turn_error`). Spending is server-pushed on the bus (`spending` envelopes from the emitter) — the 60s client poll is gone. (`web/src/hooks/useSessionStatus.ts`, `web/src/hooks/useTurnWatchdog.ts`, `web/src/hooks/useSessionStatus.test.tsx`, `web/src/hooks/useTurnWatchdog.test.tsx`, `web/src/stores/chatStore.tsx`, `web/src/components/common/StatusBar.tsx`, `web/src/components/Status/StatusPanel.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/CoworkSidebar.tsx`, `web/src/App.tsx`)
- **Per-Project Git Status Endpoint** — `GET /api/git/status` accepts a `?project=` path restricted to registered project roots (unknown paths rejected, so the endpoint can't probe arbitrary dirs) via a shared `gitStatusForDir` helper also used by the subscriber-aware server-push git watcher. (`internal/server/handler_git.go`, `internal/server/handler_git_test.go`)
- **Debug Log Sequence Cursor** — `debuglog` gained a monotonic per-entry sequence with `Cursor()`/`SnapshotSince()`, so consumers can diff correctly across ring-buffer drops (a count-based diff would stall once the ring stops growing) and across clears (no re-emits). The `logBusForwardLoop` uses it to publish each new log entry on the bus exactly once. (`internal/debuglog/debuglog.go`, `internal/debuglog/debuglog_test.go`, `internal/server/handler_logs.go`)
- **`read` Output Language from File Extension** — `TurnParts` derives the Shiki language hint for `read` tool output from the file extension in the tool call's `path` parameter (e.g. `.go` → `go`) instead of a fixed language, falling back to plain text when the path has no extension. (`web/src/components/Chat/TurnParts.tsx`)
- **Web Thinking Budget Control** — The web/desktop frontend can now display and change the main model's extended-thinking budget, matching the TUI's `ctrl+d` / `/effort` reasoning levels. The canonical level table (`off`/`low`/`med`/`high`/`xhigh`/`max` = 0/1024/8000/16000/32000/65536 tokens) moved into `internal/config` (`ThinkingBudgetLevels`/`ThinkingBudgetLabels`/`ThinkingLevelIndexForBudget`), shared with the TUI so both surfaces agree on the level set. New `GET/PUT /api/config/thinking-budget` endpoints accept a level name or a raw budget (persisted via `SaveLastThinkingBudget`, with a status-snapshot push so the sidebar updates immediately), and `TUIStatus` gained a `ThinkingBudget` field — deliberately not `omitempty`, since 0 = "off" is the meaningful default — propagated through the headless snapshot and the RC-bridge merge. The web API client and TS types gained `getThinkingBudget`/`setThinkingBudget` and `thinking_budget`. (`internal/config/ocodeconfig.go`, `internal/config/ocodeconfig_test.go`, `internal/server/handler_config.go`, `internal/server/handler_tui_status.go`, `internal/server/handler_tui_status_test.go`, `internal/server/server.go`, `internal/server/tui_status.go`, `internal/tui/model.go`, `web/src/api/client.ts`, `web/src/api/types.ts`, `web/dist/index.html`)

### Fixed
- **Bash Prefix Bans Enforced Inside Loop and Conditional Bodies** — `parseShellCommandLine` now splits on the shell reserved words that introduce a new command body without an intervening operator (`do`, `then`, `else`, `elif`), so a banned prefix rule (e.g. `rm`) is matched when it appears as the leading word of a body fragment (`for i in 1 2; do rm -rf …; done`, `if …; then rm -rf …; fi`) instead of being swallowed as a trailing word of the keyword's own fragment. (`internal/agent/permissions.go`, `internal/agent/permissions_test.go`)
- **TUI Question Dialog Esc Resets Prompt State** — pressing `esc` in the question dialog now calls `clearQuestionPrompt()` (resetting the active question's tool-call id, prompts, tab, selections, and input) instead of only hiding the dialog, so stale question state can't leak into a later prompt. (`internal/tui/other_modals_adapter.go`)
- **Web/Desktop Sessions No Longer Stall While Another Session Is Running** — Two independent chokepoints made a second session sit idle whenever one session was busy. (1) **Server:** `Handler.mu` — a map lock that every endpoint takes, including the run-state polls behind the desktop dock badge — was still held across slow work in three places: agent construction (`InitBuiltinTools`, `LoadExternalTools`, a local-model auto-start, and the unbounded `mcpCache.wait()`), and the whole compaction LLM call in `HandleCompactSession`. Any of those froze every other session's turn for their full duration. Agent construction now happens with no handler lock held (`buildAgentSession` + a double-checked `registerAgentSession` that shuts down the loser of a construction race), and compaction/recap run under the per-session lock only. (2) **Client:** the send endpoints held their HTTP request open for the entire turn, so each running session pinned one of the browser's six-per-origin HTTP/1.1 connections (plain HTTP, no h2 multiplexing) alongside the session mirror and agent-run streams — a second session's POST could simply never leave the browser. `POST /api/chat` and `POST /api/sessions/{id}/message` accept `"async": true` (the web client always sets it) and reply `202` as soon as the turn is dispatched; all turn output already flows over the SSE mirror, which is where the UI renders it from. Non-async callers (the scheduler, Telegram, external API clients) keep the old synchronous contract. Also fixed alongside: the permission/question resolvers read `agentSession.messages` under `Handler.mu` instead of the session's own lock — a data race against a running turn that async dispatch makes far more likely — now via a shared `findPendingSession` helper that returns the matched session with its lock held, without ever taking a session lock while holding `Handler.mu` (that order deadlocks against the title generator). (`internal/server/agent_session.go`, `internal/server/handler.go`, `internal/server/handler_sse.go`, `internal/server/handler_questions.go`, `internal/server/handler_permissions_resolve.go`, `internal/server/server.go`, `internal/server/agent_session_test.go`, `web/src/api/client.ts`, `web/src/hooks/useChat.ts`)
- **Self-Dispatch Guard Prevents Agents from Recursively Spawning Themselves** — `TaskTool.Execute` now refuses when the dispatching agent's own type matches the target agent spec (e.g. `explore` spawning `explore`). Unlike the re-dispatch guard which only trips after repeated identical launches, self-dispatch has no natural termination condition — each spawned instance could dispatch another, recursing without bound — so it is refused outright on the first attempt. (`internal/agent/subagent.go`)
- **File Tree and File Content Endpoints Anchor to Project Root, Not Process CWD** — `HandleFileTree` now defaults root to `h.workDir` (the server's project directory) instead of `"."` (the process CWD), fixing a bug where a Finder-launched desktop `.app` starts with cwd `/` and the file tree listed the entire filesystem. A new `depth` query param (default 4, `0` = unlimited) replaces the hardcoded cap of 4, letting the file picker fetch deep paths. Explicit `?path=` is confined to `workDir` via a new `containedIn` security check (EvalSymlinks + Rel prefix). `HandleFileContent` also resolves relative paths against `workDir`. (`internal/server/handler_files.go`, `internal/server/handler_files_test.go`)
- **Chat Panel Initial Fetch No Longer Wipes Mirror-Populated State** — `ChatPanel` now dispatches `MARK_INITIALIZED` when the initial history fetch finds that the SSE mirror already populated the session slice, instead of overwriting newer live state with the older disk snapshot. This prevents a race where switching to a tab mid-stream wiped the in-progress messages. (`web/src/components/Chat/ChatPanel.tsx`, `web/src/stores/chatStore.tsx`)
- **Permission Resolution Matches Request ID to Prevent Dialog Flicker** — `PERMISSION_RESOLVED` now carries `requestId` so the reducer only clears the matching pending permission, not any pending permission. Previously a resolved event for an earlier permission could close the dialog for a newer one that arrived during the same Step(). (`web/src/stores/chatStore.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/SessionTabSync.tsx`)
- **`rm -rf` Outside Allowed Scope Now Requires Approval in Every Mode** — `Decide` parses each bash command's shell fields and, for `rm` invocations carrying `-f`/`--force` and/or `-r`/`-R`/`--recursive`, resolves every target against the workdir and returns ASK (with an "rm" permission request) when any target resolves outside the allowed scope — regardless of YOLO mode or a persisted `rm` bash-prefix allow rule. Previously only the exact literal `rm -rf /` was hard-blocked, so YOLO mode unconditionally ALLOWed `rm -rf ../`, `rm -rf ~`, and `rm -rf /absolute/path` with no scope check at all. (`internal/agent/permissions.go`, `internal/agent/permissions_test.go`)
- **Orphaned Local Chat Servers Reclaimed Instead of Blocking the Port** — When an ocode process exits (quit, crash, SIGKILL) while a local model server it spawned is still booting, the server survives as an orphan holding its port but never answering `/v1/models`; `StartLocalModelInstance`'s kill-on-failure cleanup never runs because the process that would run it is gone. Every later start then failed its probe, could not bind the port, and burned the full 900-attempt health poll before the circuit breaker took over — the observed `local/bonsai-8b-1bit` symptom (an orphan from a dead session sat on 11458 for 15 hours returning empty replies, so the auto-permission model could never come up). New `reapStrayChatServer` runs inside `StartModelInstance` after the cross-process start lock is acquired (holding the lock is what makes a bound-but-unhealthy port a stray rather than a sibling's in-flight spawn): it identifies the holder by command line via gopsutil — requiring BOTH the served model identity (`ExpectedServeID`) and the exact `--port N` flag, so nothing else on the machine can match — then SIGTERMs it, escalates to SIGKILL, and confirms the port is bindable again. A port held by an unidentifiable process is reported with an actionable error instead of being killed or polled for 15 minutes. Before killing anything the port is re-probed once, so a healthy server that merely missed the caller's 2-second probe window (queued behind the concurrency limiter, disk pressure) is never mistaken for a stray. `acquireChatStartLock` now writes the owner pid into the lock file and a contender reclaims immediately when that owner is no longer running — without this the reap was unreachable for the first 50 minutes after an orphaning, because the dead holder's own lock file sent every contender down the `waitForChatHealth` adopt path (`chatStartLockStaleAfter`) instead of the spawn path. Not paired with a kill-on-exit hook: local servers are deliberately adoptable across ocode processes (`StartModelInstance`'s probe-and-adopt path), so killing them at shutdown would tear down a server another live session is using. (`internal/discovery/instance_reap.go`, `internal/discovery/instance_reap_test.go`, `internal/discovery/instances.go`)
- **Unreachable Permission Model No Longer Reported as a Denial** — `askPermissionModel`/`askPermissionModelInterpreter`/`consultPermissionModel` now return a trailing `consulted` flag that separates "the judge decided DENY" from "no verdict was ever obtained" (no model configured, `EnsureLocalModelRunning` failure, client creation failure, transport failure, interpreter source unreadable). Previously both collapsed into `(false, reason)`, so a dead local judge (e.g. `local/bonsai-8b-1bit` failing its health poll) produced a "⛔ Auto-denied by LLM permission model" banner for ordinary commands, and — worse — the `PermissionDeny` tier returned a hard `denied: … was denied by the LLM permission model` tool result instead of the policy denial, making a Deny-tier call auto-permission was meant to be able to override unappealable and blaming an LLM that never ran. The Ask tier now sets the new `PermissionRequest.ModelUnavailable` field instead of `DenyReason` (rendered as a neutral "ℹ Permission model unavailable — asking you instead:" notice with the ordinary "Allow this action?" prompt), and the Deny tier falls through to the existing policy-denial message. (`internal/agent/agent.go`, `internal/agent/permission_interpreter.go`, `internal/agent/permissions.go`, `internal/tui/model.go`, `internal/server/handler_permissions_resolve.go`, `internal/agent/permission_verdict_test.go`, `internal/tui/model_test.go`, `web/src/api/types.ts`, `web/src/components/Chat/PermissionDialog.tsx`, `web/src/pages/SessionPage.tsx`, `web/src/stores/chatStore.tsx`)
- **GPT-5.6 Codex Models Billed at $0** — Sessions on the codex provider's GPT-5.6 family (`gpt-5.6-luna`/`sol`/`terra`) logged `[PRICING] unknown model "gpt-5.6-…" — no pricing data found; falling back to $0` and their spend resolved to $0, because the embedded `models-snapshot.json` is a gitignored build-time artifact that predates the 5.6 family until regenerated with `make models-snapshot`. The hardcoded fallback map now carries the family's models.dev prices (luna $0.20/$1.20, terra $2/$12, sol $5/$30 per million, plus cache rates) so spend is accurate even against a stale snapshot; the local snapshot was also regenerated so the registry path resolves these models directly. (`internal/pricing/modelsdev.go`, `internal/pricing/modelsdev_test.go`)
- **Web Tool Output No Longer Shows Truncation Footers** — `ToolBlock` in the web chat now strips the `[output truncated:…]` marker (the Go layer appends it so the LLM knows how to fetch the rest) before rendering tool output, mirroring the TUI's `stripTruncationFooter`. Without this the raw footer was visible noise in the chat transcript. (`web/src/components/Chat/TurnParts.tsx`)
- **Live Chat Events Routed Correctly — No More Silent Drop** — `SessionTabSync` was subscribing to the literal string `"envelope"` (the SSE frame name the server sets on the HTTP response) rather than the individual event types that `routeBusEnvelope` handles. The bus dispatches per-event-type with no wildcard, so subscribing to `"envelope"` silently dropped every live text, turn, and status event — the UI only updated via the slow reconcile/watchdog fallback, producing the "unstable streaming" symptom. A new `ROUTABLE_EVENTS` constant in `sessionEvents.ts` exports the full set of routable event names (the two process-global ones plus every session-scoped event), and `SessionTabSync` now subscribes to each individually. A regression test verifies the subscription list never falls back to `"envelope"`. (`web/src/components/Layout/SessionTabSync.tsx`, `web/src/lib/sessionEvents.ts`, `web/src/components/Layout/SessionTabSync.test.tsx`)
- **Session First Message No Longer Dropped on Start** — `SessionTabSync` now synchronously updates the `openSessionIdsRef` routing table when it receives `session_started`, before the `user_message` echo arrives on the next SSE frame. Previously the ref still held the temporary `new-*` id when the echo landed, so the very first message of a session hit the routing gate and was never shown in the transcript. (`web/src/components/Layout/SessionTabSync.tsx`)
- **Cancelled Queued Subagent Runs Wake Immediately** — `AgentRunRegistry.AcquireForRun`/`acquireCancellable` adds the run's own `Done()` channel to the queue-wait select, so a queued subagent that reaches a terminal state (e.g. via `task_cancel`) unblocks its waiter right away instead of staying parked until an unrelated holder happens to release a concurrency slot — the registry can reflect the cancellation without waiting for unrelated traffic. (`internal/agent/agent_runs.go`, `internal/agent/subagent.go`, `internal/agent/agent_runs_test.go`)
- **Cancelled Subagent Runs Force-Release Their Limiter Slot** — `CancelOwned`/`CancelAll` now call a new `forceReleaseSlot` on the run after marking it cancelled, handing its `max_concurrent_agents` slot straight back to the shared limiter pool. `Cancel()` only closes the subagent's stop channel, which `chatWithDelta` honors for in-flight LLM calls — but `Tool.Execute` takes no context, so a subagent blocked inside a plain tool call (bash, an MCP call) never observes the cancellation and its dispatch goroutine may never return, leaving the run holding its slot forever and starving every other dispatch sharing the limiter. Safe to call unconditionally: `releaseOwnSlot` is a no-op for a run that never held a slot (e.g. it was still Queued), and the run's own deferred `releaseOwnSlot()` becomes a no-op too once its goroutine eventually does unwind. (`internal/agent/agent_runs.go`, `internal/agent/agent_runs_test.go`)

- **Empty Bash Commands Rejected** — `BashTool.ExecuteStreamCtx` now returns an error when the command is empty or whitespace-only, preventing `bash -c ""` silent no-ops that could corrupt a multi-step plan. This guards against codex/responses-lite models emitting tool calls with no arguments. (`internal/tool/exec.go`, `internal/tool/exec_test.go`)
- **OpenAI Tool Call Empty Arguments Normalized, Malformed Arguments Fatal** — An absent or empty `arguments` field (what OpenAI-compatible providers emit for zero-parameter tools like `todoread`) is now normalized to `"{}"` instead of erroring — the previous error was not matched by `isRetryableLLMClientError`, so it ended the turn rather than retrying. Genuinely malformed (non-empty but unparseable) arguments remain fatal. Applied in both the chat-completions streaming path and the responses path. (`internal/agent/client.go`, `internal/agent/client_test.go`)
- **OpenCode Go GPT-5.6 Models Route Through Responses API** — GPT-5.6 models (`gpt-5.6-luna`/`sol`/`terra`) on `opencode-go` and `opencode` providers are now routed to the OpenAI Responses API (responses-lite) instead of chat/completions, fixing empty tool-call arguments that these models produce on the chat path. (`internal/agent/client.go`, `internal/agent/client_test.go`)
- **Auto-Permission LLM No Longer Overrides Hard-Blocked Bash Commands** — `Decide` now sets `HardDeny: true` on its `PermissionDecision` for hard-blocked commands (`rm -rf /`, pipe-to-shell chains) and `/ban`-listed bash prefixes, and the auto-permission LLM judge respects this flag instead of re-evaluating the command. Previously the auto-permission path only checked for hard-blocked commands by name, so a `/ban`-listed prefix that wasn't in the hard-block list could be silently overridden by the LLM judge. (`internal/agent/permissions.go`, `internal/agent/agent.go`)
- **Discovery Ranking No Longer Stalls Turns on Slow Embedders** — `RunDiscovery` now wraps the `Discover` call (which embeds the query against the corpus) in a 500ms timeout, matching the existing timeout on `Warm`. A slow local embedder or network latency during ranking previously blocked the entire turn until the first streamed token, with no timeout to fail open. (`internal/agent/discovery_glue.go`)
- **CWD in Status Snapshot Uses Server Workdir** — `buildStatusSnapshot` now prefers `h.workDir` (set by the desktop shell or `--dir` flag) over `os.Getwd()`, which can be `/` for a Finder-launched `.app` or drift from the configured workdir on a headless serve. (`internal/server/handler_tui_status.go`)

### Changed
- **Interactive Terminal Always Enabled** — The pty-backed terminal (`/api/terminal/ws` + the Terminal sub-tab) is now unconditionally on. The `terminal_enabled` config key, the sidebar on/off switch, the sub-tab visibility gate, and the enable/disable half of `GET/PUT /api/config/terminal` were removed: there is no way to turn the terminal off. `GET /api/config/terminal` still reports `available` (server auth/loopback policy), `scrollback_lines`, and `work_dir`; `PUT` now accepts only `scrollback_lines`. Security note: the terminal hands the browser a real shell in the project root, so bind the web UI to loopback or configure server credentials on any host reachable by untrusted parties — that protection (`available`) is unchanged. (`internal/config/ocodeconfig.go`, `internal/server/handler_config.go`, `internal/server/handler_terminal.go`, `web/src/components/Layout/SessionSubTabs.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/CoworkSidebar.tsx`, `web/src/hooks/useTerminalConfig.ts`)
- **Desktop Keyboard Shortcuts** — Cmd/Ctrl+W closes the active session tab (instead of the browser tab), and Cmd/Ctrl+Q shows a native confirmation dialog before quitting the app. The Wails default menu is replaced with a custom `buildAppMenu` that omits the `CloseWindow` role (which would re-bind Cmd+W to quit the whole app) and adds a quit confirmation. The frontend `useKeyboard` hook intercepts Cmd/Ctrl+W in the webview (gated on `window._wails` presence), exempting keystrokes inside the embedded terminal (`target.closest(".xterm")`). (`cmd/ocode-desktop/main.go`, `web/src/hooks/useKeyboard.ts`, `web/src/App.tsx`)
- **Syntax Highlighting in Chat** — Fenced code blocks in assistant messages and tool output (bash, write/edit/diff) now render with syntax highlighting via a new `HighlightedCode` component backed by `highlighter.ts` (uses `shiki` under the hood). `MessageBubble` detects `language-xxx` in the markdown className and passes it to `HighlightedCode`; `TurnParts` maps tool names to output languages (`bash` → `shellsession`, `write`/`edit` → `diff`). Tool result messages now carry a resolved `toolName` from the preceding assistant's `tool_calls`. (`web/src/components/Chat/HighlightedCode.tsx`, `web/src/lib/highlighter.ts`, `web/src/components/Chat/MessageBubble.tsx`, `web/src/components/Chat/TurnParts.tsx`)
- **Session Sub-Tabs Expanded** — Each session tab now supports six sub-tabs: Chat, Agents, Changes, Logs, Status, and Terminal. The `SessionSubTabId` union was extended with `"logs"`, `"status"`, and `"terminal"`. The Terminal sub-tab is always visible — the interactive terminal is always enabled (the `terminal_enabled` config was later removed). Logs and Status panels are mounted per-session (CSS-hidden when inactive) so each tab shows its own log stream and status snapshot instead of a shared global view. (`web/src/components/Layout/SessionSubTabs.tsx`, `web/src/stores/projectStore.tsx`, `web/src/App.tsx`)
- **Per-Session TUI Status** — `tuiStatus` moved from the shared `ChatState` into each `SessionSlice`, so every session tab tracks its own model, cwd, context, spending, and LSP status. The SSE `SET_TUI_STATUS` action now carries a `sessionId` and routes to the correct slice. `SessionTabSync` dispatches status events keyed by the event's `session_id` (or the active tab for legacy events without one). (`web/src/stores/chatStore.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/SessionTabSync.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/CoworkSidebar.tsx`)
- **WorkDir Override for Config Resolution** — A new `config.SetWorkDir(dir)` sets a process-wide working directory used by `findProjectConfigDir` and `FindProjectRoot` instead of `os.Getwd()`. This lets the server resolve `opencode.json` for a project opened after boot (desktop shell, web server launched from a different cwd). Passing "" reverts to `os.Getwd()`. (`internal/config/config.go`)
- **Session ID Uniqueness** — `NewSessionID` now appends a 4-byte random hex suffix (`ses_YYYY-MM-DD-HHMMSS-XXXXXXXX`) to prevent two concurrent "new chat" requests arriving in the same second from producing identical IDs, which silently merged sessions and caused the second request to block on the first's per-session mutex. (`internal/session/session.go`)
- **Terminal Config API** — `GET /api/config/terminal` reports the terminal's `available` (server auth/loopback policy), `scrollback_lines`, and `work_dir`; `PUT /api/config/terminal` persists `scrollback_lines`. There is no enable/disable toggle — the interactive terminal is always enabled (the `terminal_enabled` field was later removed). The web API client gained `getTerminalConfig` and `setTerminalScrollbackLines`. (`internal/server/handler_config.go`, `web/src/api/client.ts`)
- **Repo-Analysis Commands Extracted to Shared `commandctx` Package** — `/changes`, `/standup`, and `/review` context-gathering logic moved from `internal/tui` to `internal/commandctx`, a new package with no TUI dependency. Both the TUI and the web/desktop server (`GET /api/command-context/{name}`) now call the same code, producing byte-identical LLM prompts. The TUI's `changes.go`, `standup.go`, and `review.go` are now thin wrappers. (`internal/commandctx/commandctx.go`, `internal/server/handler_commandctx.go`, `internal/tui/changes.go`, `internal/tui/standup.go`, `internal/tui/review.go`, `internal/server/server.go`)
- **Shared LSP Manager Across Agent Sessions** — The web/desktop handler now creates one `lsp.Manager` per process (`sharedLSPManager`, sync.Once-protected) instead of spawning a new language server per session/tab. All sessions against the same project root share `gopls` etc., eliminating redundant processes. (`internal/server/handler.go`)
- **TUI Activity Status Broadcast to Web Status Bar** — The web status bar now mirrors the TUI's activity row (LLM running, active tools, active agents) in real time. `buildTUIStatusSnapshot` populates new `LLMRunning`, `ActiveTools`, and `ActiveAgents` fields; `broadcastTUIStatus` is called on stream start/end, activity changes, and LSP events. The status bar renders agent activity on a dedicated second row with running indicators. (`internal/tui/model.go`, `internal/server/tui_status.go`, `web/src/components/common/StatusBar.tsx`, `web/src/api/types.ts`, `internal/tui/model_test.go`)
- **Web Slash Commands Expanded** — The web command palette now supports `/standup`, `/changes`, `/review`, `/context`, `/lsp`, `/agents`, `/skills`, `/mcp`, `/cron`, `/small-model`, `/advisor`, and `/github`. Repo-analysis commands (`/standup`, `/changes`, `/review`) use the new `GET /api/command-context/{name}` endpoint to assemble prompts server-side, then send them verbatim through the normal chat pipeline (TUI parity). Status-list commands (`/context`, `/lsp`, `/agents`, `/skills`, `/mcp`, `/cron`) return assistant messages with the fetched data. Config commands (`/small-model`, `/advisor`) open the model dialog. (`web/src/components/Chat/commands.ts`, `web/src/api/client.ts`, `web/src/App.tsx`)
- **Session List Returns Lightweight Refs** — `ListForDir` renamed to `ListRefsForDir` and now returns `[]Ref` (id/title/timestamps only) instead of full `[]Session` with materialized messages, halving list API latency. (`internal/session/session.go`)
- **Server Anchors Working Directory on Startup** — `server.Run` now calls `srv.SetWorkDir(os.Getwd())` so relative-path endpoints (file tree, file content, git, uploads) resolve against the project root regardless of where the process was launched from. (`internal/server/server.go`)
- **TopTabs Reordered: Sessions First** — Sessions moved from the last to the first position in the main tab strip. (`web/src/components/Layout/TopTabs.tsx`)
- **Middle-Click on Session Tabs Closes Them** — `onMouseDown` handler on `OpenSessionBar` and `SessionDialog` tabs detects `button === 1` (middle-click) and closes the tab, suppressing the browser's autoscroll behavior. (`web/src/components/Layout/OpenSessionBar.tsx`, `web/src/components/Layout/SessionDialog.tsx`)
- **Web Status Bar Simplified** — Model/small-model/advisor model display removed from the status bar (accessible via model dialog instead). Agent activity indicators added on a second row. `onModelClick` prop removed. (`web/src/components/common/StatusBar.tsx`)
- **Web/Desktop Session Tab Persistence** — Open-session tab state is now persisted server-side via `GET /api/tabs` and `PUT /api/tabs` (backed by `internal/tabs.Store` writing to `tabs.json` in the global data dir), surviving desktop shell restarts where `localStorage` would be wiped by random loopback ports. A new `GET /api/projects/current` endpoint returns the saved project matching the server's working directory, auto-adding cwd to the project store when it's a real project root (`.git`, `package.json`, etc.). (`internal/tabs/tabs.go`, `internal/server/handler_tabs.go`, `internal/server/handler_current_project.go`, `internal/server/server.go`, `internal/projects/projects.go`)
- **Session List API Paginated** — `GET /api/sessions` now accepts `limit` and `offset` query parameters and returns `{ sessions: [...], total: N }` instead of a bare array. `session.Ref` gained a `CreatedAt` field populated from the ojsonl header (falling back to file mtime). (`internal/server/handler.go`, `internal/session/session.go`, `internal/session/ojsonl.go`)
- **Web Model Selector Matches TUI Ordering** — `GET /api/models` now returns models in Favorites-first, Recently-Used-second, alphabetical-third order (mirroring the TUI's `openModelPicker`), with `Favorite` and `Recent` boolean fields on each model. `POST /api/models` now records the pick in the shared recent-models list so the web/desktop selector stays in sync with the TUI. (`internal/server/handler.go`, `internal/server/handler_config.go`)
- **Web TopTabs Unified Row with Overflow Menu** — Main tabs and editor tabs now live in a single horizontally scrollable row using Radix `TabsList`/`TabsTrigger`, with a "More" overflow dropdown when the strip is too narrow. Active-tab auto-scroll (Chrome-style) and mouse-wheel horizontal scrolling. (`web/src/components/Layout/TopTabs.tsx`, `web/src/index.css`)
- **Web LogPanel Scroll & Reconnect Fixes** — `LogPanel` accepts an `active` prop for visibility-aware scroll management (saves/restores position across tab switches, jumps to bottom on first open). Reconnect now uses a counter dependency instead of a no-op `setStreaming` to actually re-fire the SSE effect. Shared agent-run SSE connections across `AgentPreview` and `AgentsPanel` to halve connection count. (`web/src/components/Logs/LogPanel.tsx`, `web/src/hooks/useAgentRuns.ts`)
- **Desktop Wails Runtime Ready Handshake** — The desktop shell's native menu `ExecJS` calls now work because the SPA manually completes the Wails runtime-ready handshake: a new `notifyWailsRuntimeReady` helper posts `wails:runtime:ready` via the already-injected `window._wails.invoke` bridge (which Wails injects unconditionally on every navigation), setting the internal `runtimeLoaded` flag so `WebviewWindow.ExecJS` runs immediately instead of queuing forever. The `App.tsx` effect retries until the bridge appears (bounded at 200 attempts / 5 seconds). The native Settings menu item now dispatches a plain DOM `CustomEvent("ocode:open-settings")` via `ExecJS` instead of `window.EmitEvent` (which structurally cannot reach the page since the SPA is served from ocode's own HTTP server, not Wails' asset server). (`web/src/lib/wails.ts`, `web/src/App.tsx`, `cmd/ocode-desktop/main.go`)
- **Makefile `install-desktop` Builds Desktop App** — The `install-desktop` target now also runs the `desktop-app` step (macOS .app bundling) before installing, so `make install-desktop` produces a runnable `.app` in one pass. (`Makefile`)
- **Version Bump** — 0.8.48 → 0.8.49: interactive terminal always enabled (`terminal_enabled` config removed), desktop keyboard shortcuts (Cmd/Ctrl+W, Cmd/Ctrl+Q), syntax highlighting, per-session sub-tabs, terminal config API, Wails runtime ready handshake, subdirectory instruction injection. (`internal/version/version.go`)
- **Task Output Contracts (`expected_output`)** — A `task` dispatch may carry an optional `expected_output` describing the required shape/content of the child's result. When set, the child's final output is verified against the contract before being returned, with a single in-place retry on failure. The contract resolution hierarchy is: call-supplied `expected_output` wins over the agent definition's `expected_output:` frontmatter; neither present → verification skipped entirely (zero added cost). Verdicts are recorded on the `AgentRun` and surfaced in `agent_status`/`task_status`, the TUI agent strip + detail view, and the web Agents tab (DTO field `contract`). (`internal/agent/task_contract.go`, `internal/agent/subagent.go`, `internal/agent/agent_runs.go`, `internal/agent/prompts/task_verifier.txt`, `internal/server/handler_runs.go`, `internal/tui/detail_view.go`, `web/src/api/types.ts`, `web/src/components/Agents/RunNode.tsx`)
- **In-Batch Task DAG (`id` / `depends_on`)** — Parallel `task` batches may declare an in-batch DAG instead of flat fan-out. Only `task`/`agent` calls participate (`isDAGEligibleCall`). Validation rejects empty id with depends_on, duplicate ids, self-edges, unknown ids, cycles, and depends_on naming a `run_in_background` node. A wave scheduler driven by an in-degree map ensures predecessors resolve before dependents acquire a concurrency slot. Dependency output is prepended to the child's context. Failed nodes cause transitive dependents to skip. Cancellation is cooperative and per-edge (not graph-global abort). Bus lifecycle brackets the entire DAG. (`internal/agent/task_dag.go`, `internal/agent/task_dag_test.go`, `internal/agent/task_dag_review_test.go`)
- **Web Agents Panel: Session from Chat Store + Loading/Missing-Run States** — `AgentsPanel` drops its `session` prop and resolves the session from the chat store (the same single source the agent-preview rail uses), so a run clicked in the preview is always looked up in the exact tree that produced it. `useAgentRuns` now returns `{ runs, loaded }`, where `loaded` flips true once the SSE stream's first snapshot arrives (the server sends an initial frame on connect even with zero runs); until then an empty tree is a placeholder, not "no runs". The panel shows a "Loading agent run…" state while a selected run's tree hasn't arrived (instead of falling through to the list or the empty state, which made the click look like it did nothing), and surfaces a "no longer available in the current session's run list" view with a Back-to-all-runs button when the selected run isn't in the loaded tree — e.g. after registry pruning or a session switch — instead of silently showing an unrelated list. (`web/src/components/Agents/AgentsPanel.tsx`, `web/src/hooks/useAgentRuns.ts`, `web/src/components/Chat/AgentPreview.tsx`, `web/src/App.tsx`, `web/src/components/Agents/AgentsPanel.test.tsx`, `web/src/components/Chat/AgentPreview.test.tsx`, `web/dist/index.html`)
- **Permission-Model Picker Lists Enabled Local Models** — the `/permissions model` picker now appends a "Local Models" section listing enabled registered local models (e.g. `local/qwen3-4b-instruct-4bit`) — the primary, expected target for the auto-permission judge — alongside the cloud model list; disabled entries are omitted because they cannot serve permission decisions until enabled via `/localmodel enable`, so showing them would be a dead end. `handlePermissionModelCmd` now returns the picker-open command instead of discarding it, so the picker actually opens asynchronously when no model argument is given. (`internal/tui/picker.go`, `internal/tui/model.go`)
- **Cross-Project `.ojsonl` Resume: Both ID Forms Searched** — `Load` and `findOjsonlSessionAnyProject` now try both the bare session id and the canonical prefixed form when locating a `.ojsonl` file, via the new shared `sessionCandidateIDs` helper (also used by `sessionLoadPaths`), closing the last gap where a session stored under one id form could not be resumed by the other from a different cwd. (`internal/session/session.go`, `internal/session/session_test.go`, `TODO.md`)
- **Cross-Project Session Resume Removed** — `Load` no longer falls back to other projects' session directories: `shouldSearchOtherProjects`, `readSessionFileAnyProject`, and `findOjsonlSessionAnyProject` are deleted, so a session id missing from the current project's storage dir now fails with not-found instead of silently loading a session created under a different project root. Sessions must stay scoped to the project root they were created under; the cross-project resume tests are inverted accordingly (`TestLoadDoesNotFallBackToOtherProjects` asserts Load fails cross-project, and the bare-ID variant is dropped). This reverses the earlier "Cross-Project `.ojsonl` Resume" entry above. (`internal/session/session.go`, `internal/session/session_test.go`, `TODO.md`)
- **Local Model Warm-Up After Health-Ready** — once `waitForChatHealth` confirms a freshly spawned local chat server, a fire-and-forget warm-up completion request (`{"messages":[{"role":"user","content":"hi"}],"max_tokens":1}`, up to the client's 60s timeout) is issued off the caller's critical path so `/localmodel list`'s RSS memory figure (OS process RSS) reflects the model actually resident rather than just the server process having bound its port. This matters most for the MLX backend: `mlx_lm.server`'s arrays are lazily evaluated, so a freshly-spawned server has loaded only the weight graph and RSS stays near the bare Python/MLX interpreter footprint until the first forward pass forces evaluation; llama.cpp loads eagerly, so the call is effectively a no-op there, but issuing it uniformly keeps the reported number consistent and meaningful across every backend and platform without any OS/backend-specific memory-accounting API. The request is cancelled when its instance is stopped or replaced (`warmupCancel`), and failures are logged, never fatal — a slow first load must not stall health-ready reporting. (`internal/discovery/instances.go`)
- **Qwen3-4B-Instruct-4bit Local Auto-Permission Judge** — new `local/qwen3-4b-instruct-4bit` manifest (Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507, 4-bit MLX quant, darwin/arm64, ~3GB peak VRAM), evaluated as a replacement auto-permission judge for `local/bonsai-8b-1bit`: the 1-bit Bonsai quant fabricated banned-prefix matches that were not present in the command (e.g. claiming `sed -i` on a plain git push), while Qwen3-4B-Instruct-4bit reproduced none of those hallucinations across a 7-case suite built from real auto-permission session history, at ~1.2s/judgment (~19x faster than the -Thinking variant, which spends its budget on `<think>` reasoning before the verdict line). (`internal/discovery/manifest.go`)
- **Auto-Permission Elapsed-Time Debug Logging** — `askPermissionModel` and `askPermissionModelInterpreter` now emit `PERMISSION` debug rows (`tier=auto_llm_elapsed` / `tier=auto_interp_elapsed`) carrying tool name, language/source-mode, model label, verdict, consulted flag, and elapsed duration, so slow auto-permission judgments are diagnosable from the debug panel without instrumenting a request by hand. (`internal/agent/agent.go`, `internal/agent/permission_interpreter.go`)
- **Subagent Limiter Hardening (Access-Discipline Comment + Race Stress Test)** — `MaxConcurrentAgents` gains a comment documenting the access invariant (the live limit is enforced by the mutex-protected `AgentRunRegistry.SetMaxConcurrent`; the field is a plain int for JSON and written only from the queue-gated TUI event loop, so subagent construction reads can never overlap a write), and a new `-race` stress test (`TestAgentRunQueuedLifecycleRaceStress`) hammers queued-run admission, `CancelOwned` on queued runs, `PruneCompleted`, and live `SetMaxConcurrent` resizes from many goroutines at once. (`internal/config/ocodeconfig.go`, `internal/agent/agent_runs_test.go`)
- **No Bash Prefixes Banned by Default** — The default `sed` deny (`PermissionDeny`) seeded in `NewPermissionManager` is removed: bash-prefix bans are now strictly opt-in via `/ban add <prefix>`, matching how every other ban has always worked. The `/ban` help text and README no longer claim a `(default: sed)`; the `sed` special-casing in compound-command parsing is unchanged and applies to any sed rule a user configures. Tests updated to assert an empty default ban list and to exercise the manual add path (`TestPermissions_NoDefaultBashBan`, `TestPermissions_ManualBashBanIncludesSed`). This reverses the earlier "Default `sed` Ban" entry below. (`internal/agent/permissions.go`, `internal/agent/permissions_test.go`, `internal/tui/model_test.go`, `internal/tui/commands.go`, `README.md`)
- **Version Bump** — 0.8.39 → 0.8.42. (`internal/version/version.go`)
- **Agent-Crew Review Fixes (13)** — Code-review pass over the task-contract / task-DAG / persistent-todo change set. **DAG:** the result write-back indexed `dagMsgs` by the `resp.ToolCalls` index instead of the `parallelCalls` position, assigning results to the wrong tool-call slots and panicking on any batch where the two differ; the gate parsed `id`/`depends_on` off *every* parallel tool call, so `agent_status`/`bash_output`/`kill_shell` (all `Parallel()` with a required `id`) routed no-task batches through the scheduler and collided on the duplicate-id rule — both now gated by `isDAGEligibleCall`; a validation error no longer cancels unrelated parallel calls (non-task calls are dispatched normally, the error is reported only on the subagent positions); failure propagation is scoped to a node's transitive successors instead of a graph-global abort flag that skipped independent nodes nondeterministically; a `depends_on` naming a `run_in_background` node is rejected at validation rather than releasing the dependent against a "state: running" placeholder; and DAG results now carry `Notice`/`DisplayContent` via the shared `shapeToolResult` helper the legacy fan-out also uses. **Todo store:** `assignTodoIDs` ran before the destructive guard, stamping positional ids that made every old id appear to survive, so a same-length replace silently destroyed completed items — ids are now assigned after the guard, inheriting the matching item's id so a reorder keeps `t1` pointing at the same item; the advisory file lock covered only the read, leaving the revision check and write outside it (two processes could both write revision N+1) — the whole read→verify→write sequence is now one lock hold; `stripTodoHeader`'s second `TrimPrefix` could never match, leaving a `N)` residue above the sidebar list on every frame; legacy headerless content from `restoreTodoState` made `todoread` hard-fail on every call and caused `baseItems` to skip the destructive guard (new `parseTodoContent` accepts both shapes); a corrupt file rendered as "No todo list yet" instead of an explicit unreadable message; and a failed snapshot `Backup` is now logged rather than discarded. **Client:** an absent/empty tool-call `arguments` field is normalized to `"{}"` instead of failing the whole turn — it is what providers emit for zero-parameter tools (`todoread`, `plan_exit`), and the error was not matched by `isRetryableLLMClientError`, so it ended the turn rather than being retried as its comment claimed; genuinely malformed (non-empty, unparseable) arguments still error. (`internal/agent/agent.go`, `internal/agent/task_dag.go`, `internal/agent/client.go`, `internal/tool/todo_store.go`, `internal/agent/task_dag_review_test.go`, `internal/tool/todo_review_test.go`, `internal/agent/client_test.go`)
- **Models.dev Registry Tracking Metadata + Audit History** — The runtime cache (`models.json`) now stores explicit tracking metadata instead of relying on file mtime alone: `fetched_at` (when the copy was last refreshed from models.dev), `fetch_source` (`auto` TTL refresh vs `force` picker refresh), `fetch_duration_ms`, a `content_hash` (sha256 of the provider map, so any process can verify its in-memory copy matches the cache), and `model_count`. The embedded snapshot gains `model_count` too. A new bounded append-only audit log, `models-history.jsonl` (cap 5000 entries ≈ 17 days at one fetch per 5-min window per process), records every models.dev fetch attempt — success (source, model count, duration, hash) and failure (source, error) — written under the same cross-process refresh lock that serializes the fetches, so entries never interleave. Exposed read-only via `agent.RegistryHistory(limit)` (best-effort, skips torn lines, safe to call while another process appends). The cache file format is backward compatible: bare-map caches written by older binaries still load, so a mixed fleet of binaries can share the file. (`internal/agent/models_registry.go`, `tools/gen-models-snapshot/main.go`, `internal/agent/models_registry_test.go`)
- **Models.dev Registry Auto-Refresh (5-min TTL) + Cross-Process Refresh Lock** — The embedded `models-snapshot.json` is no longer trusted forever: it now carries a `generated_at` stamp (written by `tools/gen-models-snapshot`), and `loadRegistry` treats it like any other cached copy — fresh only within `modelsCacheTTL` (5 min), then refreshed from models.dev. Source order is now env override → on-disk cache (fresh) → snapshot (fresh) → guarded fetch, so a stale build-time snapshot can no longer shadow a newer cache written by any ocode process (previously the snapshot won unconditionally, the live fetch was effectively unreachable, and models released after the snapshot — e.g. the GPT-5.6 codex family — silently billed $0). Refreshes run under a cross-process advisory lock (`models.json.lock` next to the cache; flock on Unix, LockFileEx on Windows — auto-released if the holder crashes): only one process fetches at a time, contenders wait up to `registryLockWait` (5s) and then re-check the shared cache instead of fetching, and each process throttles fetch attempts to one per TTL window (`lastFetchAttempt`) so a down network or slow holder can't stall lookups. A fresh in-memory registry also re-checks the cache mtime on every load and adopts a newer cross-process cache immediately (no 5-minute stale window between processes), and cache writes are now atomic (temp file + rename) so concurrent readers never see partial JSON. The model-picker `ctrl+r` force refresh keeps always-fetch semantics but is serialized on the same lock. (`internal/agent/models_registry.go`, `internal/agent/registry_lock_unix.go`, `internal/agent/registry_lock_windows.go`, `tools/gen-models-snapshot/main.go`, `internal/agent/models_registry_test.go`, `internal/agent/agent_test.go`, `internal/agent/compact_test.go`)
- **Headless SSE Streams Tagged with Session ID** — Every broadcast event on the headless server path (web/desktop with no RC bridge) now carries `SessionID` transport metadata, emitted as the standard SSE `id` field so event payloads stay backwards-compatible — letting a shared mirror route deltas, tools, questions, permissions, and `turn_done` to the correct session tab. A `session_started` frame (with `session_id` + `request_id`) is broadcast when a chat turn creates a new session, so a temporary web tab can correlate its real session id before model callbacks begin, and agent-step failures now broadcast an `error` frame instead of only returning HTTP 500. RC-bridge events lacking a session id are decorated with the bridge's session at the mirror boundary, and the per-session `?session=` filter on `/api/chat/messages` applies to live frames. The web SPA consolidates this in the new `SessionTabSync` component: one persistent, unfiltered mirror subscription routes events to the active session (tab switches no longer open a fresh EventSource), with snapshot suffix/prefix matching for deduplication on reconnect. (`internal/server/handler.go`, `internal/server/handler_sse.go`, `internal/server/handler_questions.go`, `internal/server/handler_permissions_resolve.go`, `web/src/components/Layout/SessionTabSync.tsx` (new), `web/src/api/client.ts`)
- **Compaction Runtime Defaults: 20k Recent-Tail Cap + Small-Model Preference** — The default `keep_recent_tokens` budget is now a flat 20k tokens (previously 25% of the model window clamped to 20k–120k), bounding a long single-user-turn agentic run to a small working context. Auto-compaction now prefers the configured small model for summary generation when `small_model_enabled` is on (falling back to the main client), and `startCompactAsync` skips entirely when no LLM client is connected. (`internal/agent/agent.go`, `internal/agent/compact_test.go`)
- **Local Chat Health-Poll Circuit Breaker** — After two consecutive health-poll failures for a model ID, `StartModelInstance` now fails fast instead of spawning/polling again for 10 minutes, preventing repeated multi-minute hangs (e.g. the bonsai-8b-1bit incident where a missing native dependency caused the server to hang rather than error). Retries after the first failure use a shorter poll budget (60 attempts vs 900) via `chatBreakerRecentFailure`. A successful health check or adopt clears the counter. (`internal/discovery/instances.go`, `internal/discovery/instances_test.go`)
- **Message Picker Cursor Starts at Bottom** — `openMessagePicker` now initializes `pickerIndex` to the last item (most recent message) instead of `0`, so the picker opens at the most recent entry rather than scrolling through the entire history from the top. (`internal/tui/picker.go`)
- **Local Model Liveness Hardening (Dead-Server Detection)** — `NewClient` for the `local/` provider no longer trusts the in-memory instance record's cached "ready" state: it uses the record only to shortcut the port, then always confirms liveness with `ProbeModelInstance`, and rewrites the request's `model` field to the manifest's `ExpectedServeID` so `mlx_lm.server` doesn't try to dynamically load a literal id like "bonsai-8b-1bit" (failing with "Cannot find an appropriate cached snapshot folder ... offline" on a perfectly healthy server). `askPermissionModel`/`askPermissionModelInterpreter` now block on new `EnsureLocalModelRunning` before building a client, so a crashed-after-ready or still-cold-starting auto-permission model can't burn its retry budget on a dead port (concurrent starts are safe — they just wait on the same health-poll). `/localmodel status` (`localModelInstanceInfo`) likewise live-probes instead of reporting a cached "ready" as running. (`internal/agent/client.go`, `internal/agent/agent.go`, `internal/agent/local_models.go`, `internal/agent/permission_interpreter.go`, `internal/tui/model.go`)
- **Hugging Face Token Support for MLX Chat Downloads** — New `/localmodel hf-token <token>` subcommand persists a HF access token via the auth credential store (`auth.Set("huggingface", ...)`, 0600 perms, synced through `auth.OnCredentialsSaved`). `spawnMLXChatServer` now pre-downloads the model through new `ensureMLXChatModelCached` (huggingface_hub `snapshot_download` with `local_files_only=True` first — a pure local cache check with no network re-verify per launch — falling back to a real network download only when something is actually missing), with live progress in the discovery debug log via `downloadProgressWriter` and actionable auth errors via `mlxDownloadError` pointing gated/rate-limited repos at `/localmodel hf-token`. (`internal/discovery/instances.go`, `internal/agent/local_models.go`, `internal/tui/model.go`)
- **Local Chat Health-Poll and Start-Lock Budgets Raised** — `waitForChatHealth`'s poll raised from 300 to 900 attempts (weights are downloaded before the poll starts, so the loop only covers server boot; 15 min is a safety margin for a large 1-bit model on a slow disk), and `chatStartLockStaleAfter` raised from 6 to 50 minutes so a start-lock held across both a cold download (30-min budget) and the health poll is never stolen mid-flight. (`internal/discovery/instances.go`)
- **Compaction max_tokens Explicitly Capped to the Model's Real Limit** — Summary requests now send an explicit `max_tokens` via a per-call context override (`ctxKeyMaxTokens`) instead of leaving it unset. New `ModelMaxOutputTokens(modelID)` looks up the summary model's actual completion-token cap from the models.dev registry (falling back to a conservative constant only when the model is unknown to the registry). Some OpenAI-compatible routers (e.g. opencode-go) default an omitted `max_tokens` to the model's remaining context window rather than its real completion-token cap, and reject the request once that computed default exceeds the cap — breaking compaction of large conversations (e.g. deepseek-v4-flash via opencode-go on a near-context-limit session). Applied via context rather than a `*GenericClient` field so it scopes to the summary call only, not any other request sharing the same client (the summary client is the same shared client as the main coding loop whenever no dedicated `compact.summary_provider`/`summary_model` is configured). (`internal/agent/client.go`, `internal/agent/compact.go`, `internal/agent/models_registry.go`, `internal/agent/client_test.go`, `internal/agent/models_registry_test.go`)
- **Compaction Summary Timeout Raised** — Default `summary_timeout_seconds` increased from 90s to 600s (both the config default in `defaultCompactConfig` and the runtime fallback when the value is unset). Compaction summarisation uses an inactivity-based deadline that resets on each streamed chunk, so the higher ceiling primarily protects slow first-token / long-prefill cases (large compaction inputs, local models) where the first chunk can arrive after the old 90s budget. (`internal/config/ocodeconfig.go`, `internal/agent/agent.go`)
- **Local Model Concurrency Limiter (Cross-Process Slot Gate)** — `mlx_lm.server` accepts no `--parallel`/`--max-sequences` equivalent (the `--decode-concurrency` flag doesn't exist), so nothing in the spawned process enforces the configured `max_parallel`.
